Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Takahashi is solving quizzes. He has easily solved all but the last one.
The last quiz has three choices: 1, 2, and 3.
With his supernatural power, Takahashi has found out that the choices A and B are both wrong.
Print the correct choice for this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - Each of the numbers A and B is 1, 2, or 3.
 - A and B are different.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
A
B

-----Output-----
Print the correct choice.

-----Sample Input-----
3
1

-----Sample Output-----
2

When we know 3 and 1 are both wrong, the correct choice is 2.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know that choices A and B are both wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (which must be the remaining one)

## Step-by-step Analysis

Since there are only 3 choices (1, 2, 3) and we know two of them are wrong, the correct answer must be the one that is not A or B.

For example:
- If A = 3 and B = 1, then the correct answer is 2 (since 3 and 1 are wrong)
- If A = 1 and B = 2, then the correct answer is 3 (since 1 and 2 are wrong)

## Solution Approach

1. Read the two wrong choices A and B
2. The correct choice is simply the number from {1, 2, 3} that is not equal to A or B
3. We can find this by checking which number from 1, 2, 3 is missing

## Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that is not A or B
# Since we have 1, 2, 3 and two of them are wrong, the remaining one is correct
choices = [1, 2, 3]
correct_choice = [choice for choice in choices if choice != A and choice != B][0]

print(correct_choice)
```

## Verification with Sample

Sample input: A = 3, B = 1
- choices = [1, 2, 3]
- 1 is wrong (A = 3, B = 1), so 1 is excluded
- 3 is wrong (A = 3), so 3 is excluded  
- Only 2 remains
- Output: 2 ✓

The solution works correctly for the given sample.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. Takahashi knows that choices A and B are both wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (which must be the remaining one)
4. A and B are different values from {1, 2, 3}

## Solution Approach

Since there are only 3 possible choices (1, 2, 3) and we know two of them are wrong (A and B), the correct answer must be the one that remains.

We can solve this by:
1. Reading the two wrong choices A and B
2. Finding which number from {1, 2, 3} is not equal to A or B
3. Outputting that number

## Step-by-step Implementation

1. Read A and B from standard input
2. Create a set of all possible choices {1, 2, 3}
3. Remove A and B from this set
4. The remaining element is our answer

Alternatively, we can use simple logic: the correct choice is 1+2+3 - A - B

Let me verify with the sample:
- A = 3, B = 1
- Correct answer should be 2
- 1 + 2 + 3 - 3 - 1 = 2 ✓

## Code Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The sum of all choices (1+2+3) minus the wrong choices gives us the correct one
correct_choice = 6 - A - B
print(correct_choice)
```

The solution works because:
- The sum of all possible choices (1 + 2 + 3) is 6
- Since A and B are both wrong, their sum should be subtracted from 6
- The result is the only remaining choice, which is correct

print(correct_choice)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. Choices A and B are both wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (which must be the remaining one)
4. A and B are different values (1, 2, or 3)

## Logic

Since there are only 3 choices and we know two of them are wrong (A and B), the correct answer must be the one that's not A and not B.

So we need to find the number from {1, 2, 3} that is different from both A and B.

## Step-by-step solution

1. Read the values of A and B from input
2. The correct choice will be the one that's not A and not B
3. Since there are only 3 options, the correct answer is simply the remaining number

## Implementation approach

We have three possibilities: 1, 2, 3
If A and B are given, then the correct answer is the one that's missing from these two.

For example:
- If A=3, B=1, then the missing number is 2
- If A=1, B=2, then the missing number is 3
- If A=2, B=3, then the missing number is 1

## Code

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that's not A and not B
# Since we have numbers 1, 2, 3, we can calculate the missing one
choices = [1, 2, 3]
correct_choice = [choice for choice in choices if choice != A and choice != B][0]

print(correct_choice)
```

Let me verify with the sample:
- A = 3, B = 1
- Choices: [1, 2, 3]
- 1 ≠ 3 and 1 ≠ 1? No, 1 equals B (1) → skip
- 2 ≠ 3 and 2 ≠ 1? Yes → include
- 3 ≠ 3 and 3 ≠ 1? No, 3 equals A (3) → skip
- So correct choice is 2 ✓

The solution works correctly.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know two of the choices (A and B) are wrong
3. We need to find the remaining correct choice
4. A and B are different (so they can't be the same value)

## Solution Approach

Since there are only 3 possible choices (1, 2, 3), and we know that two of them are wrong (A and B), the correct answer must be the one that's not A and not B.

We can solve this by:
1. Reading the two wrong choices A and B

3. Output that number

## Step-by-step solution

2. Read B from input
3. The correct answer is the one value in {1, 2, 3} that is not A and not B

## Implementation

A = int(input())

- 1 is wrong (matches B), 3 is wrong (matches A)
- Only 2 remains

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are three choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know that choices A and B are both wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (which must be the remaining one)

## Logic

Since there are only three choices (1, 2, 3) and we know two of them are wrong, the correct choice must be the one that is not A or B.

We can solve this by:
1. Reading the two wrong choices A and B
2. Finding which number from {1, 2, 3} is not equal to A or B

## Step-by-step solution

1. Read A and B from input
2. The correct answer is the one among {1, 2, 3} that is different from both A and B
3. We can check each number from 1 to 3 and return the one that doesn't match A or B

## Verification with sample

- Input: A=3, B=1
- Wrong choices: 3 and 1
- Remaining choice: 2
- Output: 2 ✓

## Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that is not A or B
# Since we have choices 1, 2, 3, we can check which one is missing
for choice in [1, 2, 3]:
    if choice != A and choice != B:
        print(choice)
        break
```

This solution works because:
- We iterate through all possible choices (1, 2, 3)
- We find the choice that is neither A nor B
- That choice must be the correct one since only one choice remains after eliminating A and B

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know that choices A and B are both wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (the one that's not A or B)
4. Since there are only 3 options and A and B are different, the correct answer must be the remaining number

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Identify what we know
- We have three possible choices: 1, 2, 3
- A and B are both wrong, so the correct answer is NOT A and NOT B
- Since A and B are different, there's exactly one number left that isn't A or B

### Step 2: Find the missing number
- We need to find which of the numbers 1, 2, 3 is not equal to A or B
- We can simply check which number is missing from the set {1, 2, 3}

### Step 3: Implementation approach
- Read A and B from input
- The correct answer is the number that is not A and not B
- Since we know the options are only 1, 2, 3, we can calculate it as:
  - If A is 1 and B is 2, then answer is 3
  - If A is 1 and B is 3, then answer is 2  
  - If A is 2 and B is 1, then answer is 3
  - If A is 2 and B is 3, then answer is 1
  - If A is 3 and B is 1, then answer is 2
  - If A is 3 and B is 2, then answer is 1

### Step 4: Simplified approach
We can use the fact that the sum of 1+2+3 = 6, so the correct answer is 6 - A - B.

## Code Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct answer is the one that's not A or B
# Since 1+2+3 = 6, the correct answer is 6 - A - B
correct_choice = 6 - A - B

print(correct_choice)
```

## Verification with sample:
- Sample input: A=3, B=1
- Correct choice = 6 - 3 - 1 = 2
- This matches the expected output!

The solution works because:
- The sum of all three choices (1+2+3) is 6
- If we remove A and B, the remaining number (correct choice) is 6 - A - B
- Since A and B are different and both from {1,2,3}, this will always give us the correct remaining choice

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 possible choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know that choices A and B are both wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (which must be the remaining one)

## Logic Analysis

Since there are only 3 choices and we know 2 of them are wrong, the correct choice must be the one that's not A or B.

The solution is simple: we need to find which number from {1, 2, 3} is not equal to A or B.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read the two wrong choices A and B
2. Since there are only 3 possible choices, the correct choice is the one that's missing from {A, B}
3. We can find this by checking which number from 1, 2, 3 is not equal to A or B

## Implementation Approach

- The correct answer will be the number that is neither A nor B
- We can check each possibility: if 1 ≠ A and 1 ≠ B, then 1 is correct
- Otherwise if 2 ≠ A and 2 ≠ B, then 2 is correct
- Otherwise if 3 ≠ A and 3 ≠ B, then 3 is correct

Alternatively, we can use a simpler approach: since the correct answer must be in {1, 2, 3}, we can find it by checking which one is not in the set {A, B}.

## Code Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that is not A or B
# Since there are only 3 choices, we can check each possibility
if A != 1 and B != 1:
    print(1)
elif A != 2 and B != 2:
    print(2)
else:
    print(3)
```

Wait, let me verify this logic with the sample:
- A = 3, B = 1
- A != 1 and B != 1? 3 != 1 is True, but 1 != 1 is False, so False
- A != 2 and B != 2? 3 != 2 is True, but 1 != 2 is True, so True
- This would print 2, which matches the expected output!

Actually, I can simplify this. Since A and B are different, and we need the missing one, I can use:

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct answer is the number from {1, 2, 3} that is not A or B
for choice in [1, 2, 3]:
    if choice != A and choice != B:
        print(choice)
        break
```

        break

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are three choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. Choices A and B are both wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (which must be the remaining one)
4. A and B are different (so we don't have duplicates)

## Logic

Since there are only three choices (1, 2, 3) and we know two of them (A and B) are wrong, the correct answer must be the one that's left.

We can solve this by:
- Reading the two wrong choices (A and B)
- Finding which number from {1, 2, 3} is not equal to A or B

## Step-by-step solution

1. Read A and B from input
2. The correct answer is the number that's not A and not B
3. Since we have exactly three numbers and two are wrong, the correct one is the missing one

## Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that's not A and not B
# Since we have choices 1, 2, 3, and two are wrong, the correct one is the remaining one
choices = [1, 2, 3]
correct = [choice for choice in choices if choice != A and choice != B][0]

print(correct)
```

Let me trace through the sample:
- A = 3, B = 1
- Choices: [1, 2, 3]
- Remove 3: [1, 2]
- Remove 1: [2]
- Correct answer is 2 ✓

This matches the expected output.

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

And while Mishka is enjoying her trip...

Chris is a little brown bear. No one knows, where and when he met Mishka, but for a long time they are together (excluding her current trip). However, best friends are important too. John is Chris' best friend.

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

Input

5 5 1 2
1 2
3 1
4 3
3 4
1 4


Output

5.0000000000

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a bus moving horizontally (decreasing x-coordinate) with speed v
2. A pedestrian starts at (0,0) and needs to reach (0,w) by moving vertically
3. The pedestrian can move with speed u (any speed ≤ u)
4. The pedestrian is hit if they are strictly inside the bus polygon
5. We need to find the minimum time to cross safely

## Key Insights

1. Since the pedestrian moves vertically and the bus moves horizontally, we need to find when the pedestrian is safe from collision
2. The bus moves leftward, so at time t, the bus is shifted by (-vt)
3. The pedestrian moves from (0,0) to (0,w) - if they move at speed u, the time to cross is w/u
4. We need to check if the pedestrian is ever strictly inside the bus during their journey

## Strategy

The pedestrian needs to cross such that at no moment are they strictly inside the bus. 

The key insight is that we need to find when the pedestrian can safely cross. The pedestrian is safe if at every moment t, the point (0, y) is not strictly inside the bus polygon at time t.

Since the bus moves leftward with speed v, at time t, the bus is positioned with all x-coordinates decreased by vt.

For a point (x, y) to be strictly inside a polygon, it must be strictly on the positive side of all edges (not on the boundary).

For our pedestrian at (0, y) at time t, they are strictly inside if:
- The point (0, y) is strictly inside the transformed polygon at time t

We need to find the minimum t such that the pedestrian can cross from (0,0) to (0,w) without ever being strictly inside.

## Approach

The pedestrian's path is fixed (vertical line). We need to check if they are strictly inside the bus at any point during their journey.

The critical insight is that we can use binary search on time. For a given time T, we check if the pedestrian can cross safely in time T.

For a given crossing time T, the pedestrian reaches (0,w) at time T. During this time, they spend time t in the interval [0,T].

For each position (0, y) on the path, we need to check if it's strictly inside the bus at the time when the pedestrian is at that position.

At time t when the pedestrian is at (0, y), the bus has moved left by v*t.

So we need to check if (0, y) is strictly inside the polygon with vertices shifted left by v*t, for all y in [0,w].

The pedestrian is safe if for all y in [0,w], the point (0,y) is not strictly inside the polygon.

## Correct Approach

1. The pedestrian moves from (0,0) to (0,w) at speed u. So they reach (0,w) at time T = w/u.
2. At time t (where 0 ≤ t ≤ T), the pedestrian is at position (0, y) where y = u*t.
3. At the same time t, the bus has moved left by v*t, so we need to check if (0, y) is strictly inside the polygon with vertices (x_i - v*t, y_i).

For each point (0, y) on the path, we need to check if it's strictly inside the polygon shifted left by v*t.

The pedestrian is safe if for all t in [0,T], the point (0, y(t)) is not strictly inside the polygon.

The critical observation is that the pedestrian is hit only if there's some t where (0, y(t)) is strictly inside the transformed polygon.

The answer should be the minimum T such that the pedestrian can cross without being hit.

We can use binary search on T, and for each T, check if the pedestrian can cross safely.

Actually, the minimum crossing time is w/u, but we need to check if this is safe.

If w/u is too small, the pedestrian might be hit. If it's too large, they're safe.

The pedestrian is safe if they never enter the strictly interior of the bus.

For a given time T, the pedestrian reaches (0,w) at time T. We need to check if at any time t ∈ [0,T], the point (0, y(t)) with y(t) = u*t is strictly inside the polygon with vertices (x_i - v*t, y_i).

To check if a point is strictly inside a polygon, we can check if it's on the positive side of all edges.

## Implementation Steps

2. For a given time T, check if pedestrian can cross safely

```python
def point_in_polygon(point, polygon):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    p1x, p1y = polygon[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = polygon[i % n]
        if point[1] > min(p1y, p2y):
            if point[1] <= max(p1y, p2y):
                if point[0] <= max(p1x, p2x):
                    if p1y != p2y:
                        xinters = (point[1] - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
                    if p1x == p2x or point[0] <= xinters:
                        inside = not inside
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    return inside

def is_point_strictly_inside(point, polygon):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(polygon)
    count = 0
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = polygon[i]
        x2, y2 = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the same side of edge
        cross_product = (point[0] - x1) * (y2 - y1) - (point[1] - y1) * (x2 - x1)
        
        # If cross product is 0, point is on edge or vertex
        if cross_product == 0:
            return False  # On edge, not strictly inside
        
        # Check if point is on the positive side of edge
        if cross_product > 0:
            count += 1
    
    # Point is strictly inside if count is odd (for counter-clockwise polygon)
    return count % 2 == 1

def point_inside_polygon(point, vertices):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon with counter-clockwise vertices"""
    n = len(vertices)
    inside = False
    
    p1x, p1y = vertices[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = vertices[i % n]
        # Check if point is on the left side of edge
        cross_product = (point[0] - p1x) * (p2y - p1y) - (point[1] - p1y) * (p2x - p1x)
        if cross_product > 0:
            inside = not inside
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    # For counter-clockwise polygon, strictly inside means it's on the inside of every edge
    # The standard ray casting algorithm: if odd number of intersections, inside
    return inside

```python
import sys

def cross_product(p1, p2, p3):
    """Cross product of vectors (p2-p1) and (p3-p1)"""
    return (p2[0] - p1[0]) * (p3[1] - p1[1]) - (p2[1] - p1[1]) * (p3[0] - p1[0])

def point_in_polygon_strict(point, vertices):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon (not on edge)"""
    n = len(vertices)
    inside = False
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = vertices[i]
        x2, y2 = vertices[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the edge
        cross_prod = cross_product((x1, y1), (x2, y2), point)
        if cross_prod == 0:
            return False  # on edge, not strictly inside
            
        # For counter-clockwise polygon, if cross_prod > 0, point is on the left side
        if cross_prod > 0:
            inside = not inside
    
    return inside

def is_pedestrian_hit(time, vertices, w, v, u):
    """Check if pedestrian is hit during crossing in time 'time'"""
    # The pedestrian takes time to cross, but might need to wait
    # For the given time, we need to check if they are strictly inside at any moment
    
    # At time t (0 <= t <= time), pedestrian is at (0, u*t)
    # Bus vertices are at (x_i - v*t, y_i)
    
    # We need to check if at any t in [0, time], the point (0, u*t) is strictly inside the shifted polygon
    
    # To save computation, we check at critical points
    # Check at t = 0, t = time, and at t = time/2
    
    # Check at t=0
    shifted = [(x - v*0, y) for x, y in vertices]
    if point_in_polygon_strict((0, 0), shifted):
        return True
        
    # Check at t=time
    t_final = time
    shifted = [(x - v*t_final, y) for x, y in vertices]
    if point_in_polygon_strict((0, u*t_final), shifted):
        return True
        
    # Check at t = time/2
    t_mid = time / 2
    shifted = [(x - v*t_mid, y) for x, y in vertices]
    if point_in_polygon_strict((0, u*t_mid), shifted):
        return True
        
    # Also check if any edge of the shifted polygon intersects x=0 within the height range
    # For each edge, check if the line between (x1,y1) and (x2,y2) intersects x=0
    # and if the intersection point is within y in [0,w]
    
    for i in range(len(vertices)):
        j = (i + 1) % len(vertices)
        x1, y1 = vertices[i]
        x2, y2 = vertices[j]
        
        # Line from (x1,y1) to (x2,y2)
        # Parametric form: x = x1 + t*(x2-x1), y = y1 + t*(y2-y1)
        # Set x=0: 0 = x1 + t*(x2-x1) => t = -x1/(x2-x1)
        
        if x1 == x2:
            continue  # vertical line
            
        t_intersect = -x1 / (x2 - x1)
        
        # Check if t_intersect is in [0,1]
        if 0 <= t_intersect <= 1:
            y_intersect = y1 + t_intersect * (y2 - y1)
            # Check if y_intersect in [0, w]
            if 0 < y_intersect < w:
                # The pedestrian might be at this point at time t = y_intersect / u
                t_ped = y_intersect / u
                if 0 <= t_ped <= time:
                    # Check if at time t_ped, the point is strictly inside
                    # We need to verify this
                    shifted = [(x - v * t_ped, y) for x, y in vertices]
                    if point_in_polygon_strict((0, y_intersect), shifted):
                        return True
    
    return False

# Read input
line = input().split()
n, w, v, u = int(line[0]), int(line[1]), int(line[2]), int(line[3])

vertices = []
for _ in range(n):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    vertices.append((x, y))

# We need to find minimum time T such that the pedestrian is not hit
# Binary search on T

# Upper bound: in worst case, pedestrian might need to wait until the bus passes, then cross
# The bus moves left at speed v, so if it needs to pass, the time might be up to w/u + some value
# We can set upper bound to, say, 10**9

low = 0.0
high = 10**10

# The answer is at least w/u, at most something larger
# We know w/u is a lower bound for crossing time, but might not be safe

# We'll binary search for the minimum T such that is_pedestrian_hit(T) is False
# (i.e., pedestrian is not hit)

# But is_pedestrian_hit(T) should be False when safe

# However, I need to adjust: we want the minimum T where the pedestrian is safe
# So we want the smallest T such that is_pedestrian_hit(T) is False

# But for small T, they might be hit, for large T they are safe.

# Actually, for larger T, the bus has moved further left, so the pedestrian might be safe.

# So we want the first T where they are not hit.

# But is_pedestrian_hit(T) is True if they are hit at some time in [0,T]

# We want to find the minimum T such that is_pedestrian_hit(T) is False

# This is wrong because for T=0, they are at (0,0), which might be on boundary.

# Actually, we want the minimum T such that they are never strictly inside.

# For very large T, they will be safe.

# So we do binary search for T where is_pedestrian_hit(T) is False.

# However, the sample: answer is 5.0
# w/u = 2.5

# At T=2.5, is_pedestrian_hit(2.5) should be True (they are hit)
# At T=5.0, is_pedestrian_hit(5.0) should be False

# Let's implement binary search.

epsilon = 1e-9

while high - low > epsilon:
    mid = (low + high) / 2.0
    if not is_pedestrian_hit(mid, vertices, w, v, u):
        high = mid
    else:
        low = mid

print(f"{low:.10f}")

        x2, y2 = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the same side of edge
        cross_product = (point[0] - x1) * (y2 - y1) - (point[1] - y1) * (x2 - x1)
        
        # If cross product is 0, point is on edge or vertex
        if cross_product == 0:
            return False  # On edge, not strictly inside
        
        # Check if point is on the positive side of edge
        if cross_product > 0:
            count += 1
    
    # Point is strictly inside if count is odd (for counter-clockwise polygon)
    return count % 2 == 1

def point_inside_polygon(point, vertices):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon with counter-clockwise vertices"""
    n = len(vertices)
    inside = False
    
    p1x, p1y = vertices[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = vertices[i % n]
        # Check if point is on the left side of edge
        cross_product = (point[0] - p1x) * (p2y - p1y) - (point[1] - p1y) * (p2x - p1x)
        if cross_product > 0:
            inside = not inside
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    # For counter-clockwise polygon, strictly inside means it's on the inside of every edge
    # The standard ray casting algorithm: if odd number of intersections, inside
    return inside

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

And while Mishka is enjoying her trip...

Chris is a little brown bear. No one knows, where and when he met Mishka, but for a long time they are together (excluding her current trip). However, best friends are important too. John is Chris' best friend.

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

Input

5 5 1 2
1 2
3 1
4 3
3 4
1 4


Output

5.0000000000

We can check for each edge of the polygon whether the vertical line x=0 intersects it at some time t.

For edge between vertices (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) at time 0, at time t, the vertices become (x1 - v*t, y1) and (x2 - v*t, y2).

The parametric form of this edge is: (x,y) = (x1 - v*t + s*(x2 - x1 - v*t), y1 + s*(y2 - y1)) for s ∈ [0,1].

We need to find if there's t and s such that x=0 and y = (w/T)*t.

So:
x = x1 - v*t + s*(x2 - x1 - v*t) = 0
y = y1 + s*(y2 - y1) = (w/T)*t

From the second equation: s = ( (w/T)*t - y1 ) / (y2 - y1)

From the first equation: x1 - v*t + s*(x2 - x1 - v*t) = 0

Substituting s:
x1 - v*t + [((w/T)*t - y1)/(y2 - y1)] * (x2 - x1 - v*t) = 0

This gives us a quadratic equation in t. If it has a solution in [0,T] and s ∈ [0,1], then the pedestrian is hit.

We can do this for each edge and check if there's any intersection.

If there's no such intersection for any edge, then the pedestrian is safe at time T.

1. Binary search on T from w/u to some upper bound
2. For each T, check if the pedestrian is ever hit by solving for intersections with each edge
3. Return the minimum T where the pedestrian is not hit

import sys
import numpy as np

def point_in_polygon(point, polygon):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon (not on boundary)"""
    x, y = point
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    p1x, p1y = polygon[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = polygon[i % n]
        if y > min(p1y, p2y):
            if y <= max(p1y, p2y):
                if x <= max(p1x, p2x):
                    if p1y != p2y:
                        xinters = (y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
                    if p1x == p2x or x <= xinters:
                        inside = not inside
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    return inside

def is_point_inside(px, py, vertices, t, v):
    """Check if point (px, py) is strictly inside polygon at time t"""
    # Move vertices by -v*t in x direction
    moved_vertices = [(x - v * t, y) for x, y in vertices]
    return point_in_polygon((px, py), moved_vertices)

def is_hit_in_time(T, W, U, vertices, v):
    """Check if pedestrian is hit during time T given total time T"""
    # Total time T >= W/U for pedestrian to complete crossing
    if T < W / U:
        return True
    
    # Pedestrian goes from (0,0) to (0,W)
    # At time t (0 <= t <= T), height is h = (W / T) * t
    # Check if at any t, (0, h) is strictly inside the bus at time t
    
    # We only need to check critical points - when the pedestrian's path intersects edges
    # For each edge of the polygon, find if there's intersection with vertical line x=0 at some time t
    
    n = len(vertices)
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = vertices[i]
        x2, y2 = vertices[j]
        
        # Edge at time 0: from (x1,y1) to (x2,y2)
        # At time t: from (x1-v*t, y1) to (x2-v*t, y2)
        
        # Parametric form: 
        # x = x1 - v*t + s*(x2 - x1 - v*t) = 0
        # y = y1 + s*(y2 - y1) = (W/T)*t
        
        # From second equation: s = ( (W/T)*t - y1 ) / (y2 - y1)
        # If y2 == y1, then only check if y1 == (W/T)*t
        if y2 == y1:
            # Horizontal edge, y is constant
            if y1 != (W / T) * t:  # This doesn't make sense with t
                continue
            # For horizontal edge at height y1:
            # We need to check if x=0 intersects the edge
            # The edge spans from x1-v*t to x2-v*t
            # So if 0 is in (x1-v*t, x2-v*t) then intersection
            # For a specific t, we can check
            # Actually we need to solve for t
            pass
        
        # Instead, let's just check a few critical times
        # The critical times are when the pedestrian's height matches the edge's y-coordinate
        # and when the intersection happens
        
        # Solve: y1 + s*(y2 - y1) = (W/T)*t
        # and x1 - v*t + s*(x2 - x1 - v*t) = 0
        
        # From first equation: s = ( (W/T)*t - y1 ) / (y2 - y1)
        # Substitute into second:
        # x1 - v*t + [((W/T)*t - y1)/(y2 - y1)] * (x2 - x1 - v*t) = 0
        
        # Multiply by (y2 - y1):
        # (x1 - v*t) * (y2 - y1) + ((W/T)*t - y1) * (x2 - x1 - v*t) = 0
        
        # Expand:
        # x1*(y2 - y1) - v*t*(y2 - y1) + (W/T)*t*(x2 - x1) - y1*(x2 - x1) - v*t*(W/T)*t + y1*(x2 - x1) + v*t*y1 = 0
        
        # Simplify:
        # x1*(y2 - y1) - v*t*(y2 - y1) + (W/T)*t*(x2 - x1) - v*t*(W/T)*t = 0
        
        # This is a quadratic in t: A*t^2 + B*t + C = 0
        # where A = -v*(W/T), B = -v*(y2-y1) + (W/T)*(x2-x1), C = x1*(y2-y1)
        
        A = -v * (W/T)
        B = -(y2 - y1) * v + (W/T) * (x2 - x1)
        C = x1 * (y2 - y1)
        
        # If A == 0, it's linear
        if abs(A) < 1e-9:
            if abs(B) < 1e-9:
                if abs(C) < 1e-9:
                    # Degenerate case
                    continue
                else:
                    return True
            else:
                t_val = -C / B
                if 0 <= t_val <= T:
                    # Check if s is in [0,1]
                    y_val = y1 + ((W/T)*t_val - y1) / (y2 - y1) * (y2 - y1)
                    s_val = ( (W/T)*t_val - y1 ) / (y2 - y1)
                    if 0 <= s_val <= 1:
                        return True
        else:
            discriminant = B*B - 4*A*C
            if discriminant < 0:
                continue
            
            sqrt_disc = discriminant**0.5
            t1 = (-B - sqrt_disc) / (2*A)
            t2 = (-B + sqrt_disc) / (2*A)
            
            for t_val in [t1, t2]:
                if t_val < 0 or t_val > T:
                    continue
                    
                # Check if s in [0,1]
                s_val = ( (W/T)*t_val - y1 ) / (y2 - y1)
                if s_val < 0 or s_val > 1:
                    continue
                    
                # Verify the point (0, (W/T)*t_val) is strictly inside
                # We need to check if at time t_val, (0, (W/T)*t_val) is strictly inside
                # But this is for the edge intersection
                
                # The pedestrian is hit if at time t_val, (0, h) is strictly inside
                h = (W/T) * t_val
                # Check if (0,h) is strictly inside with the moved vertices
                moved_vertices = [(x - v*t_val, y) for x, y in vertices]
                # Use ray casting to check if strictly inside
                x, y = 0, h
                inside = False
                p1x, p1y = moved_vertices[0]
                for i in range(1, len(moved_vertices) + 1):
                    p2x, p2y = moved_vertices[i % len(moved_vertices)]
                    if y > min(p1y, p2y):
                        if y <= max(p1y, p2y):
                            if x <= max(p1x, p2x):
                                if p1y != p2y:
                                    xinters = (y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
                                if p1x == p2x or x <= xinters:
                                    inside = not inside
                    p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
                
                if inside:
                    return True
    
    return False

def solve():
    line = input().split()
    n, w, v, u = int(line[0]), int(line[1]), int(line[2]), int(line[3])
    
    vertices = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        vertices.append((x, y))
    
    # Binary search on time
    # Minimum time is w/u (if pedestrian moves at speed u)
    low = w / u
    high = 10**12  # Large upper bound
    
    # We need to find minimum T such that pedestrian is NOT hit
    # If T is too small, pedestrian is hit (moves too fast)
    # If T is large enough, pedestrian is not hit
    
    # Binary search for smallest T where pedestrian is safe
    # We'll check if pedestrian is hit at time T
    eps = 1e-9
    
    for _ in range(100):  # 100 iterations gives precision ~1e-30
        mid = (low + high) / 2
        if is_hit_in_time(mid, w, u, vertices, v):
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid
    
    print(f"{low:.10f}")

def point_in_polygon_robust(px, py, polygon):
    """Check if point (px, py) is strictly inside polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    p1x, p1y = polygon[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = polygon[i % n]
        if py > min(p1y, p2y):
            if py <= max(p1y, p2y):
                if px <= max(p1x, p2x):
                    if p1y != p2y:
                        xinters = (py - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
                    if p1x == p2x or px <= xinters:
                        inside = not inside
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    return inside

def check_safe(T, w, u, vertices, v):
    """Check if pedestrian is safe to cross in time T"""
    # Pedestrian speed limit: w/T <= u, so T >= w/u
    if T < w / u:
        return False
    
    # At time t (0 <= t <= T), pedestrian is at height: h = w * t / T
    # We need to check if at any t, point (0, h) is strictly inside the bus at time t
    # The bus at time t has vertices (x_i - v*t, y_i)
    
    # We'll check critical points - intersections with edges
    n = len(vertices)
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = vertices[i]
        x2, y2 = vertices[j]
        
        # Edge at time 0: from (x1, y1) to (x2, y2)
        # At time t: from (x1 - v*t, y1) to (x2 - v*t, y2)
        
        # We want to find if there's a time t and parameter s such that:
        # (0, h) lies on this edge where h = w*t/T
        
        # Parametric equations:
        # x = x1 - v*t + s*(x2 - x1 - v*t)
        # y = y1 + s*(y2 - y1)
        # h = w*t/T
        
        # From y equation: s = (h - y1) / (y2 - y1) = (w*t/T - y1) / (y2 - y1)
        
        # Substitute into x equation:
        # 0 = x1 - v*t + [(w*t/T - y1)/(y2 - y1)] * (x2 - x1 - v*t)
        
        # Multiply by (y2 - y1):
        # 0 = (x1 - v*t) * (y2 - y1) + (w*t/T - y1) * (x2 - x1 - v*t)
        
        # Expand:
        # 0 = x1*(y2-y1) - v*t*(y2-y1) + w*t*(x2-x1)/T - y1*(x2-x1) - v*t*(w*t/T) + y1*(x2-x1) + v*t*y1
        # 0 = x1*(y2-y1) - v*t*(y2-y1) + w*t*(x2-x1)/T - v*t*(w*t/T)
        
        # Simplify:
        # 0 = x1*(y2-y1) + t * [ -v*(y2-y1) + w*(x2-x1)/T - v*w*t/T ]
        
        # This is a quadratic in t: A*t^2 + B*t + C = 0
        # where A = -v*w/T, B = -v*(y2-y1) + w*(x2-x1)/T, C = x1*(y2-y1)
        
        A = -v * w / T
        B = -v * (y2 - y1) + w * (x2 - x1) / T
        C = x1 * (y2 - y1)
        
        # If A == 0, it's linear
        if abs(A) < 1e-12:
            if abs(B) < 1e-12:
                if abs(C) < 1e-12:
                    continue  # degenerate case
                else:
                    return True
            else:
                t_val = -C / B
                if 0 <= t_val <= T:
                    # Check if s in [0,1]
                    h_val = w * t_val / T
                    s_val = (h_val - y1) / (y2 - y1)
                    if 0 <= s_val <= 1:
                        # Verify with polygon check
                        moved_vertices = [(x - v*t_val, y) for x, y in vertices]
                        if point_in_polygon_robust(0, h_val, moved_vertices):
                            return True
        else:
            discriminant = B*B - 4*A*C
            if discriminant < 0:
                continue
                
            sqrt_disc = discriminant**0.5
            t1 = (-B - sqrt_disc) / (2*A)
            t2 = (-B + sqrt_disc) / (2*A)
            
            for t_val in [t1, t2]:
                if t_val < 0 or t_val > T:
                    continue
                    
                h_val = w * t_val / T
                s_val = (h_val - y1) / (y2 - y1)
                if s_val < 0 or s_val > 1:
                    continue
                    
                # Check if (0, h_val) is strictly inside the bus at time t_val
                moved_vertices = [(x - v*t_val, y) for x, y in vertices]
                if point_in_polygon_robust(0, h_val, moved_vertices):
                    return True
    
    return False

def point_in_polygon_robust(px, py, polygon):
    """Check if point (px, py) is strictly inside polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    p1x, p1y = polygon[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = polygon[i % n]
        if py > min(p1y, p2y):
            if py <= max(p1y, p2y):
                if px <= max(p1x, p2x):
                    if p1y != p2y:
                        xinters = (py - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
                    if p1x == p2x or px <= xinters:
                        inside = not inside
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    return inside

def check_safe(T, w, u, vertices, v):
    """Check if pedestrian is safe to cross in time T"""
    # Pedestrian speed limit: w/T <= u, so T >= w/u
    if T < w / u:
        return False
    
    # At time t (0 <= t <= T), pedestrian is at height: h = w * t / T
    # We need to check if at any t, point (0, h) is strictly inside the bus at time t
    # The bus at time t has vertices (x_i - v*t, y_i)
    
    # We'll check critical points - intersections with edges
    n = len(vertices)
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = vertices[i]
        x2, y2 = vertices[j]
        
        # Edge at time 0: from (x1, y1) to (x2, y2)
        # At time t: from (x1 - v*t, y1) to (x2 - v*t, y2)
        
        # We want to find if there's a time t and parameter s such that:
        # (0, h) lies on this edge where h = w*t/T
        
        # Parametric equations:
        # x = x1 - v*t + s*(x2 - x1 - v*t)
        # y = y1 + s*(y2 - y1)
        # h = w*t/T
        
        # From y equation: s = (h - y1) / (y2 - y1) = (w*t/T - y1) / (y2 - y1)
        
        # Substitute into x equation:
        # 0 = x1 - v*t + [(w*t/T - y1)/(y2 - y1)] * (x2 - x1 - v*t)
        
        # Multiply by (y2 - y1):
        # 0 = (x1 - v*t) * (y2 - y1) + (w*t/T - y1) * (x2 - x1 - v*t)
        
        # Expand:
        # 0 = x1*(y2-y1) - v*t*(y2-y1) + w*t*(x2-x1)/T - y1*(x2-x1) - v*t*(w*t/T) + y1*(x2-x1) + v*t*y1
        # 0 = x1*(y2-y1) - v*t*(y2-y1) + w*t*(x2-x1)/T - v*t*(w*t/T)
        
        # Simplify:
        # 0 = x1*(y2-y1) + t * [ -v*(y2-y1) + w*(x2-x1)/T - v*w*t/T ]
        
        # This is a quadratic in t: A*t^2 + B*t + C = 0
        # where A = -v*w/T, B = -v*(y2-y1) + w*(x2-x1)/T, C = x1*(y2-y1)
        
        A = -v * w / T
        B = -v * (y2 - y1) + w * (x2 - x1) / T
        C = x1 * (y2 - y1)
        
        # If A == 0, it's linear
        if abs(A) < 1e-12:
            if abs(B) < 1e-12:
                if abs(C) < 1e-12:
                    continue  # degenerate case
                else:
                    return True
            else:
                t_val = -C / B
                if 0 <= t_val <= T:

                    h_val = w * t_val / T
                    s_val = (h_val - y1) / (y2 - y1)
                    if 0 <= s_val <= 1:
                        # Verify with polygon check
                        moved_vertices = [(x - v*t_val, y) for x, y in vertices]
                        if point_in_polygon_robust(0, h_val, moved_vertices):
                            return True
        else:
            discriminant = B*B - 4*A*C
            if discriminant < 0:
                continue
                
            sqrt_disc = discriminant**0.5
            t1 = (-B - sqrt_disc) / (2*A)
            t2 = (-B + sqrt_disc) / (2*A)

Chris is a little brown bear. No one knows, where and when he met Mishka, but for a long time they are together (excluding her current trip). However, best friends are important too. John is Chris' best friend.

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

1 2
3 1

3 4
1 4

5.0000000000

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a bus moving leftward (decreasing x) with speed `v`
2. A pedestrian starts at (0,0) and needs to reach (0,w) by moving vertically
3. The pedestrian can move at speed `u` (any speed ≤ u)
4. The pedestrian is hit if they're strictly inside the bus polygon (not on edges or vertices)
5. We need to find the minimum time to cross safely

## Key Insights

1. Since the pedestrian moves vertically and the bus moves horizontally, we need to find when the bus will "pass over" the pedestrian's position at each y-coordinate.

2. For a pedestrian to cross safely, at each y-coordinate (from 0 to w), the time it takes for the pedestrian to reach that y-coordinate must be greater than the time it takes for the bus to reach that y-coordinate such that the bus is still "in front" of the pedestrian.

3. For a given y-coordinate, let's say the pedestrian reaches it at time `t_y`. At that time, the bus's x-coordinate at that y has changed by `-v * t_y`.

4. The pedestrian is safe if at any point in time, they are not strictly inside the bus polygon.

5. The critical insight is that we need to find the minimum time such that for every y-coordinate, when the pedestrian reaches it, the bus hasn't reached that y-coordinate. Actually, we need to check when the bus "crosses" the pedestrian's path.

```python
def point_in_polygon(point, polygon):
    """Check if point is strictly inside the polygon (not on edges or vertices)"""
    x, y = point
    n = len(polygon)
    
    # Count intersection with edges
    intersections = 0
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = polygon[i]
        x2, y2 = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on edge or vertex
        if (x == x1 and y == y1) or (x == x2 and y == y2) or (x == x1 and x == x2 and y >= min(y1, y2) and y <= max(y1, y2)):
            return False  # On vertex or edge
            
        # Check if point is strictly inside
        if (y1 > y and y2 <= y) or (y2 > y and y1 <= y):
            if x < max(x1, x2):
                if x >= min(x1, x2):
                    # Intersects, but need to check if strictly inside
                    intersections += 1
    
    # If odd number of intersections, point is inside (assuming simple polygon)
    return intersections % 2 == 1

def transformed_point_in_polygon(point, polygon, t, v, w):
    """Check if point (0, y) is strictly inside the polygon at time t"""
    x, y = point
    y_transformed = y
    x_transformed = x - v * t
    
    # Transform the polygon by decreasing x-coordinates by v*t
    transformed_polygon = []
    for px, py in polygon:
        transformed_polygon.append((px - v * t, py))
    
    # Check if (x_transformed, y_transformed) is strictly inside transformed_polygon
    # Use ray casting algorithm
    n = len(transformed_polygon)
    intersections = 0
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = transformed_polygon[i]
        x2, y2 = transformed_polygon[j]
        
        # Skip if point is at vertex or on edge
        if (x_transformed == x1 and y_transformed == y1) or (x_transformed == x2 and y_transformed == y2):
            return False
            
        # Check if point is on edge (in x or y)
        if (x_transformed <= max(x1, x2) and x_transformed >= min(x1, x2) and 
            y_transformed >= min(y1, y2) and y_transformed <= max(y1, y2)):
            return False
            
        # Check intersection
        if (y1 > y_transformed and y2 <= y_transformed) or (y2 > y_transformed and y1 <= y_transformed):
            # Calculate intersection x-coordinate
            denom = (x2 - x1)
            if denom == 0:
                continue
            t_intersect = (y_transformed - y1) * (x2 - x1) / (y2 - y1)
            if x_transformed < max(x1, x2) and x_transformed > min(x1, x2):
                if t_intersect >= min(x1, x2) and t_intersect <= max(x1, x2):
                    intersections += 1
    
    return intersections % 2 == 1

def is_safe(T, n, w, v, u, polygon):
    """Check if pedestrian can cross safely in time T"""
    # Check each time point t where pedestrian reaches height y = u*t
    # But we only need to check critical points
    
    # The pedestrian reaches heights at times t = y/u for y in [0, w]
    # We need to check if at any t in [0, T], (0, u*t) is strictly inside the polygon at time t
    
    # Since the pedestrian moves continuously, we can check critical times
    # But for simplicity, we can check if there's any t where the point is strictly inside
    
    # We need to check if there exists any t in [0, T] such that (0, u*t) is strictly inside polygon at time t
    for t in range(0, T + 1):
        y = u * t
        if y > w:
            break
            
        # Check if (0, y) is strictly inside polygon at time t
        x = 0
        # Transform polygon by decreasing x by v*t
        transformed_polygon = []
        for px, py in polygon:
            transformed_polygon.append((px - v * t, py))
            
        # Check if (x, y) is strictly inside transformed_polygon
        n_poly = len(transformed_polygon)
        intersections = 0
        
        for i in range(n_poly):
            j = (i + 1) % n_poly
            x1, y1 = transformed_polygon[i]
            x2, y2 = transformed_polygon[j]
            
            # Skip if point is on vertex or edge
            if (x == x1 and y == y1) or (x == x2 and y == y2):
                return False
                
            if (x >= min(x1, x2) and x <= max(x1, x2) and 
                y >= min(y1, y2) and y <= max(y1, y2)):
                # On edge - not strictly inside
                return False
            
            # Check intersection with edge
            if (y1 > y and y2 <= y) or (y2 > y and y1 <= y):
                # Calculate intersection x
                denom = x2 - x1
                if denom == 0:
                    continue
                t_intersect = (y - y1) * denom / (y2 - y1)
                if x < max(x1, x2) and x > min(x1, x2):
                    if t_intersect >= min(x1, x2) and t_intersect <= max(x1, x2):
                        intersections += 1
        
        # If even number of intersections, point is outside
        if intersections % 2 == 0:
            continue
        else:
            return False
    
    return True

def solve():
    n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
    polygon = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        polygon.append((x, y))
    
    # Binary search on time T
    # Minimum possible T is 0, maximum is something large
    # The pedestrian must reach (0,w), so minimum time is w/u
    # But they might need to wait to avoid being hit
    
    left, right = 0.0, w / u + 1e9
    
    # We need to find minimum T such that the pedestrian is safe
    # The pedestrian is safe if at no time t in [0, T] is (0, u*t) strictly inside the polygon at time t
    
    # Actually, we need to find when the pedestrian first becomes unsafe
    # So we binary search for the minimum T such that the pedestrian is safe
    
    # But let's reconsider: the pedestrian can go to (0,w) in time w/u
    # If they go in time w/u, they might be hit at some point
    
    # We need to find the minimum T such that there's no t in [0, T] where (0, u*t) is strictly inside
    # The answer is the minimum such T
    
    # Binary search until we find safe T
    for _ in range(100):  # 100 iterations for precision
        mid = (left + right) / 2
        if is_safe(mid, n, w, v, u, polygon):
            right = mid
        else:
            left = mid
    
    print(f"{left:.10f}")

```python
def is_point_inside(polygon, x, y):
    """Check if point (x,y) is strictly inside the polygon"""
    n = len(polygon)
    intersections = 0
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = polygon[i]
        x2, y2 = polygon[j]
        
        # Skip if on edge or vertex
        if x == x1 and y == y1:
            return False
        if x == x2 and y == y2:
            return False
            
        # Check if point is on edge
        if (x >= min(x1, x2) and x <= max(x1, x2) and 
            y >= min(y1, y2) and y <= max(y1, y2)):
            return False
        
        # Check intersection
        if (y1 > y and y2 <= y) or (y2 > y and y1 <= y):
            denom = x2 - x1
            if denom != 0:
                t = (y - y1) * denom / (y2 - y1)
                if x < max(x1, x2) and x > min(x1, x2):
                    if t >= min(x1, x2) and t <= max(x1, x2):
                        intersections += 1
    
    return intersections % 2 == 1

```python
def is_inside(polygon, x, y):
    n = len(polygon)
    intersections = 0
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = polygon[i]
        x2, y2 = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on edge or vertex
        if x == x1 and y == y1:
            return False
        if x == x2 and y == y2:
            return False
            
        # Check if point is on edge (in x or y)
        if (x >= min(x1, x2) and x <= max(x1, x2) and 
            y >= min(y1, y2) and y <= max(y1, y2)):
            return False
        
        # Check intersection
        if (y1 > y and y2 <= y) or (y2 > y and y1 <= y):
            denom = x2 - x1
            if denom != 0:
                t = (y - y1) * denom / (y2 - y1)
                if x < max(x1, x2) and x > min(x1, x2):
                    if t >= min(x1, x2) and t <= max(x1, x2):
                        intersections += 1
    
    return intersections % 2 == 1

def is_safe(T, n, w, v, u, polygon):
    # Check if pedestrian can cross safely in time T
    # Pedestrian reaches height y at time y/u for y in [0, w]
    # At time t = y/u, the bus has moved left by v*(y/u)
    # We need to check if (0, y) is strictly inside the transformed polygon
    
    for y in range(0, w + 1):
        if y > w:
            break
        t = y / u
        if t > T:
            break
            
        # Transform polygon by decreasing x by v*t
        transformed_polygon = []
        for px, py in polygon:
            transformed_polygon.append((px - v * t, py))
            
        # Check if (0, y) is strictly inside transformed polygon
        if is_inside(transformed_polygon, 0, y):
            return False
    
    return True

n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())

But this has issues with floating point and range. Let me fix it:

```python
def ray_casting_inside(polygon, x, y):
    """Check if point (x,y) is strictly inside the polygon using ray casting"""
    n = len(polygon)
    intersections = 0
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = polygon[i]
        x2, y2 = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on edge or vertex
        if x == x1 and y == y1 or x == x2 and y == y2:
            return False
            
        # Check if point is on the edge segment
        if (max(x1, x2) >= x >= min(x1, x2) and 
            max(y1, y2) >= y >= min(y1, y2)):
            return False
            
        # Check if ray from (x,y) hits the edge
        if (y1 > y and y2 <= y) or (y2 > y and y1 <= y):
            # Calculate intersection x-coordinate
            denom = x2 - x1
            if denom == 0:
                continue
            t = (y - y1) * denom / (y2 - y1)
            if min(x1, x2) < t < max(x1, x2):
                intersections += 1
    
    return intersections % 2 == 1

n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
polygon = []
for _ in range(n):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    polygon.append((x, y))

# Binary search on time T
left, right = 0.0, 10**10

# We need to find minimum T such that for all times t in [0,T], 
# when pedestrian is at (0, u*t), they are not strictly inside the bus polygon at time t
for _ in range(100):
    mid = (left + right) / 2
    
    # Check if safe in time mid
    safe = True
    for y in range(0, w + 1):
        if y / u > mid:
            break
            
        t = y / u
        # Transform polygon by moving left by v*t
        transformed_polygon = [(px - v * t, py) for px, py in polygon]
        
        # Check if (0, y) is strictly inside transformed polygon
        if ray_casting_inside(transformed_polygon, 0, y):
            safe = False
            break
    
    if safe:
        right = mid
    else:
        left = mid

print(f"{left:.10f}")

            return False

            return False

                t = (y - y1) * denom / (y2 - y1)

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

And while Mishka is enjoying her trip...

Chris is a little brown bear. No one knows, where and when he met Mishka, but for a long time they are together (excluding her current trip). However, best friends are important too. John is Chris' best friend.

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

Input

5 5 1 2
1 2
3 1
4 3
3 4
1 4


Output

5.0000000000

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

Input

5 5 1 2
1 2
3 1
4 3
3 4
1 4


Output

5.0000000000

```python
import sys
from typing import List, Tuple

def cross_point_in_polygon(point: Tuple[float, float], polygon: List[Tuple[float, float]]) -> bool:
    """Check if point lies strictly inside polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    p1x, p1y = polygon[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = polygon[i % n]
        if point[1] > min(p1y, p2y):
            if point[1] <= max(p1y, p2y):
                if point[0] <= max(p1x, p2x):
                    if p1y != p2y:
                        xints = (point[1] - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
                    else:
                        xints = min(p1x, p2x)
                    if point[0] < xints:
                        inside = not inside
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    return inside

def point_in_convex_polygon(point: Tuple[float, float], polygon: List[Tuple[float, float]]) -> bool:
    """Check if point lies strictly inside convex polygon"""
    n = len(polygon)
    if n == 0:
        return False
    
    # Check if point is strictly inside (not on boundary)
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = polygon[i]
        x2, y2 = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the edge
        if (point[0] == x1 and point[1] == y1) or (point[0] == x2 and point[1] == y2):
            return False  # on vertex
            
        # Check if point is strictly inside
        cross_product = (point[0] - x1) * (y2 - y1) - (point[1] - y1) * (x2 - x1)
        if cross_product == 0:  # collinear
            # Check if point lies on segment
            if min(x1, x2) <= point[0] <= max(x1, x2) and min(y1, y2) <= point[1] <= max(y1, y2):
                return False  # on edge
        
    # For convex polygon, if not on boundary, check one side
    return True

def point_inside_polygon(point: Tuple[float, float], polygon: List[Tuple[float, float]]) -> bool:
    """Check if point lies strictly inside polygon using raycasting"""
    x, y = point
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    p1x, p1y = polygon[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = polygon[i % n]
        
        # Check if point is on the edge or vertex
        if (x == p1x and y == p1y) or (x == p2x and y == p2y):
            return False
            
        # Check if point is on the edge
        if (min(p1y, p2y) < y < max(p1y, p2y)) and (x <= max(p1x, p2x)) and (x >= min(p1x, p2x)):
            # Check if collinear
            if y == p1y + (p2y - p1y) * (x - p1x) / (p2x - p1x) if p2x != p1x else p1y:
                return False
                
        # Ray casting - if point is strictly inside
        if (p1y > y) != (p2y > y):
            if x < max(p1x, p2x):
                if y <= max(p1y, p2y):
                    if y >= min(p1y, p2y):
                        if x >= min(p1x, p2x):
                            inside = not inside
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    return inside

# Actually, let me implement the proper solution using binary search

def solve():
    n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
    vertices = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        vertices.append((x, y))
    
    # If the pedestrian can cross immediately and the bus is not a threat
    # or if the bus is too far away, they can cross in time w/u
    
    # But we need to check if they can avoid being hit
    
    # Binary search on the total time T
    # For a given T, check if it's possible to cross safely within time T
    
    def safe_to_cross(T):
        # The pedestrian needs to go from (0,0) to (0,w) in time T
        # At time t, they are at position (0, y) where y = min(u * t, w)
        
        # But they can control speed, so we can have different y at different times
        # The path is a function y(t)
        
        # For safety, for each time t in [0, T], the point (0, y(t)) should NOT lie strictly inside the shifted polygon
        
        # At time t, the bus has moved to x = -v*t
        # Check if point (0, y(t)) lies strictly inside the polygon with vertices (x_i - v*t, y_i)
        
        # Since the pedestrian can choose y(t), we need to find if there's a path
        
        # Actually, a simpler insight: the pedestrian can cross in time T if and only if 
        # there exists a continuous function y(t) such that y(0)=0, y(T)=w, and |dy/dt| <= u,
        # and for all t in [0,T], the point (0, y(t)) does NOT lie strictly inside the polygon at time t.
        
        # But this is hard to check directly.
        
        # Alternative insight: the minimum crossing time is w/u, unless there's a constraint due to the bus.

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

And while Mishka is enjoying her trip...

Chris is a little brown bear. No one knows, where and when he met Mishka, but for a long time they are together (excluding her current trip). However, best friends are important too. John is Chris' best friend.

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

Input

5 5 1 2
1 2
3 1

3 4
1 4


Output

5.0000000000
